{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_540a14504a9c45e4925b393c","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n5\t\n6\tPart of #35\n7\t\n8\t## Why\n9\t\n10\t`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n11\twithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n12\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n13\tproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\n14\tshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\n15\tcasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\n16\tthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\n17\twritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\n18\tconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n19\t\n20\tThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\n21\tto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\n22\tin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\n23\tof it.\n24\t\n25\t## What to do\n26\t\n27\tValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n28\t`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\n29\tunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\n30\tschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\n31\tproviders.\n32\t\n33\tCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\n34\tnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\n35\tresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n36\t\n37\tA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\n38\tso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n39\t`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\n40\tgeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\n41\tit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\n42\tthere is no opt-out flag.\n43\t\n44\tUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n45\t\n46\t## Acceptance criteria\n47\t\n48\t- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n49\t      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n50\t      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n51\t- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n52\t      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n53\t      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n54\t      count is `0`\n55\t- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n56\t      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n57\t      exactly once\n58\t- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n59\t      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n60\t      **not** contain the offending value\n61\t- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n62\t      that the thrown message names the tool\n63\t- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n64\t      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n65\t- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n66\t- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n67\t      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n68\t      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n69\t      round\n70\t- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n71\t      pinned by a test\n72\t- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n73\t      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n74\t      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n75\t      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n76\t      containing that field's value\n77\t- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n78\t      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n79\t      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n80\t      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n81\t- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n82\t      `devDependencies`, and\n83\t      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n84\t- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n85\t      merely present\n86\t- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n87\t      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n88\t      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n89\t      returned to the model rather than thrown\n90\t- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n91\t      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n92\t      properties pass through unmodified\n93\t- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n94\t      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n95\t      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n96\t      bump is MINOR\n97\t- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n98\t      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n99\t      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n100\t      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n101\t- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n102\t      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n103\t      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n104\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n105\t      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n106\t\n107\t## Tech notes\n108\t\n109\tToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n110\t`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\n111\tWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\n112\tthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n113\t`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n114\t\n115\tDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\n116\tclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\n117\tdirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\n118\tthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\n119\tevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\n120\tvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\n121\towns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\n122\tauthorization model); retry, timeout\n123\tand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\n124\twritten against this contract later.\n125\t\n126\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"869efe1c-1b30-454f-ace5-d4df21543193","uuid":"909fdec9-ce74-407f-b7b5-16b76784f722","timestamp":"2026-11-03T23:14:53.860Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/5d08507a0ead/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\nwithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\nEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\nproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\nshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\ncasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\nthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\nwritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\nconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n\nThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\nto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\nin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\nof it.\n\n## What to do\n\nValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\nunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\nschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\nproviders.\n\nCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\nnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\nresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n\nA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\nso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\ngeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\nit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\nthere is no opt-out flag.\n\nUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n      count is `0`\n- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n      exactly once\n- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n      **not** contain the offending value\n- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n      that the thrown message names the tool\n- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n      round\n- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n      pinned by a test\n- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n      containing that field's value\n- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n      `devDependencies`, and\n      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n      merely present\n- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n      returned to the model rather than thrown\n- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n      properties pass through unmodified\n- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n      bump is MINOR\n- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n\n## Tech notes\n\nToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\nWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\nthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n\nDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\nclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\ndirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\nthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\nevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\nvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\nowns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\nauthorization model); retry, timeout\nand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\nwritten against this contract later.\n\n","numLines":126,"startLine":1,"totalLines":126}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_540a14504a9c45e4925b393c","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{result: response} : response;\n}\n\nexport type ToolDefinitionSource = ToolDefinition | (() => ToolDefinition);\n\nexport interface RegisteredTool {\n  definition: ToolDefinition;\n  execute: ToolExecutor;\n}\n\ninterface StoredTool {\n  definitionSource: ToolDefinitionSource;\n  execute: ToolExecutor;\n}\n\nexport class ToolRegistry {\n  private readonly tools = new Map<string, StoredTool>();\n\n  private resolveDefinition(source: ToolDefinitionSource): ToolDefinition {\n    return typeof source === 'function' ? source() : source;\n  }\n\n  register(definition: ToolDefinitionSource, execute: ToolExecutor): void {\n    const resolved = this.resolveDefinition(definition);\n    this.tools.set(resolved.name, {definitionSource: definition, execute});\n  }\n\n  get(name: string): RegisteredTool | undefined {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) return undefined;\n    return {definition: this.resolveDefinition(tool.definitionSource), execute: tool.execute};\n  }\n\n  getDefinitions(): ToolDefinition[] {\n    return [...this.tools.values()].map(t => this.resolveDefinition(t.definitionSource));\n  }\n\n  getPromptInstructions(): string[] {\n    return this.getDefinitions()\n      .filter(t => t.promptInstructions)\n      .map(t => `${t.name}: ${t.promptInstructions}`);\n  }\n\n  getExamplePrompts(): string[] {\n    return this.getDefinitions().flatMap(t => t.examplePrompts ?? []);\n  }\n\n  has(name: string): boolean {\n    return this.tools.has(name);\n  }\n\n  async execute(name: string, input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext): Promise<ToolResponse> {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) {\n      log.warn('tool', 'tool not found', {name});\n      throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${name}`);\n    }\n    log.info('tool', 'executing', {name, inputKeys: Object.keys(input)});\n    const rawResult = await tool.execute(input, context);\n    const response = normalizeToolResponse(rawResult);\n    log.info('tool', 'execution complete', {\n      name,\n      rawType: typeof rawResult,\n      clientMessageCount: response.clientMessages?.length ?? 0,\n      clientMessageTypes: response.clientMessages?.map(m => m.type) ?? {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_bbc7895baba5444b8a6542a1","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Definition of Done\n2\t\n3\t> `ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n4\t> without ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n5\t>\n6\t> A tool that receives unexpected input must fail in one predictable, known way before the rest\n7\t> of this epic's tool work can be built on top of it.\n8\t\n9\t**Strategy: `direct`** — the seam already exists. `ToolRegistry.register`/`execute` and\n10\t`createChatOrchestrator(...).processMessage` are the real entry points and are called directly\n11\twith real registries; the only fixture is a scripted AI provider, the same pattern\n12\t`src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts` already uses. No new module or boundary is needed.\n13\t\n14\t## Done when these pass\n15\t\n16\tAll in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`. Six are red today because the\n17\tbehaviour is absent; two are green today **by design** — the ticket names them as pins that\n18\tconstrain the fix (pass-through must survive the change, the unknown-tool throw must stay a\n19\tthrow), and there is no honest way to make existing behaviour fail.\n20\t\n21\t- [ ] **rejects a call missing a required property, naming it, without invoking the executor** —\n22\t      red today (`executorCalls: 1`, result carries no mention of `location`/`required`).\n23\t      Covers acceptance criteria 1 and 2.\n24\t      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n25\t- [ ] **passes accepted input through to the executor unchanged, exactly once** — green pin\n26\t      (`toEqual` on what the executor received, so a default or coercion added by the fix fails\n27\t      it). Covers acceptance criterion 3.\n28\t      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n29\t- [ ] **rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, never the value** — red\n30\t      today. Covers acceptance criterion 4.\n31\t      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n32\t- [ ] **rejects a value outside an enum without leaking identifying input into result or logs** —\n33\t      red today (executor invoked, rejection text absent); also enforces the `enum` half of\n34\t      \"required, types and enum\" and the no-value-in-logs redaction rule via `setLogger`.\n35\t      Covers acceptance criteria \"enforces enum\" and 10.\n36\t      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n37\t- [ ] **refuses an uncompilable schema at registration, naming the tool** — red today\n38\t      (`register` never throws). Covers acceptance criterion 5.\n39\t      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n40\t- [ ] **validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call** — red\n41\t      today (stale pass-through). Covers acceptance criterion 6.\n42\t      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n43\t- [ ] **still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name** — green pin, deliberately out of\n44\t      scope for change. Covers acceptance criterion 9.\n45\t      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n46\t- [ ] **returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it** — red\n47\t      today (executor called twice, no rejection text in the messages the provider sees on\n48\t      round 2). Covers acceptance criterion 8. The ticket places this test in\n49\t      `chatOrchestrator.test.ts`; it lives here instead so the acceptance suite stays one file\n50\t      the Bash hook can name.\n51\t      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n52\t\n53\t## Facets\n54\t\n55\t- [ ] Red: the six tests above fail for the reason stated under each.\n56\t- [ ] Green: add `ajv` to `dependencies` (not peer/dev); compile a static tool's schema at\n57\t      `register` (broken schema throws there, message naming the tool); resolve and compile a\n58\t      function-valued source per call, cached by the resolved schema object; validate in\n59\t      `execute` before the executor; return a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names failing paths\n60\t      and constraints, never values, in both result and log lines.\n61\t- [ ] Refactor: the two green pins stay green unedited; existing registry consumers\n62\t      (`chatOrchestrator.ts`) need no change — the rejection already flows back as a tool result.\n63\t- [ ] Compile-once: the spec statement \"a static tool's schema is compiled once ... cost one\n64\t      compilation\" is deliberately uncited — compilation is unobservable through the public API,\n65\t      so it has no honest behaviour-level red bar. Six are red today because the\nbehaviour is absent; two are green today **by design** — the ticket names them as pins that\nconstrain the fix (pass-through must survive the change, the unknown-tool throw must stay a\nthrow), and there is no honest way to make existing behaviour fail.\n\n- [ ] **rejects a call missing a required property, naming it, without invoking the executor** —\n      red today (`executorCalls: 1`, result carries no mention of `location`/`required`).\n      Covers acceptance criteria 1 and 2.\n      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **passes accepted input through to the executor unchanged, exactly once** — green pin\n      (`toEqual` on what the executor received, so a default or coercion added by the fix fails\n      it). Covers acceptance criterion 3.\n      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, never the value** — red\n      today. Covers acceptance criterion 4.\n      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **rejects a value outside an enum without leaking identifying input into result or logs** —\n      red today (executor invoked, rejection text absent); also enforces the `enum` half of\n      \"required, types and enum\" and the no-value-in-logs redaction rule via `setLogger`.\n      Covers acceptance criteria \"enforces enum\" and 10.\n      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **refuses an uncompilable schema at registration, naming the tool** — red today\n      (`register` never throws). Covers acceptance criterion 5.\n      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call** — red\n      today (stale pass-through). Covers acceptance criterion 6.\n      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name** — green pin, deliberately out of\n      scope for change. Covers acceptance criterion 9.\n      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it** — red\n      today (executor called twice, no rejection text in the messages the provider sees on\n      round 2). Covers acceptance criterion 8. The ticket places this test in\n      `chatOrchestrator.test.ts`; it lives here instead so the acceptance suite stays one file\n      the Bash hook can name.\n      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Red: the six tests above fail for the reason stated under each.\n- [ ] Green: add `ajv` to `dependencies` (not peer/dev); compile a static tool's schema at\n      `register` (broken schema throws there, message naming the tool); resolve and compile a\n      function-valued source per call, cached by the resolved schema object; validate in\n      `execute` before the executor; return a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names failing paths\n      and constraints, never values, in both result and log lines.\n- [ ] Refactor: the two green pins stay green unedited; existing registry consumers\n      (`chatOrchestrator.ts`) need no change — the rejection already flows back as a tool result.\n- [ ] Compile-once: the spec statement \"a static tool's schema is compiled once ... cost one\n      compilation\" is deliberately uncited — compilation is unobservable through the public API,\n      so it has no honest behaviour-level red bar. Pin it in `registry.test.ts` only if that can\n      be done without stubbing the validator; otherwise code review judges it.\n- [ ] Doc (implementer): one-line doc comment on `execute` pointing at\n      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`; update `docs/adding-a-tool.md` (no more\n      defensive casting guidance), `README.md`, `.specify/spec.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`; PR body\n      carries the migration note and the MINOR version bump; flip the spec's `Status` row as the\n      implementation round's own tests get cited.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Returning an unknown tool name to the model instead of throwing (separate issue, named in the\n  ticket); `Unknown tool: <name>` stays a throw.\n- Validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an evaluation harness.\n- Per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still valid;\n  `beforeToolCall` is a veto seam, not an authorization model.\n- Retry, timeout and rate limiting around tool execution.\n- Any actual tool implementation, which is written against this contract later.\n","numLines":82,"startLine":1,"totalLines":82}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_bbc7895baba5444b8a6542a1","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_65362cfdfc904620a59e3b40","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport {createChatOrchestrator} from '../chatOrchestrator.js';\n2\timport {ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\n3\timport type {ToolExecutor, ToolResponse} from './registry.js';\n4\timport type {AIProvider, MessageChunk, SendMessageParams} from '../../types/ai.js';\n5\timport type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\n6\timport {PromptBuilder} from '../../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\n7\timport {setLogger} from '../../shared/logger.js';\n8\timport type {Logger} from '../../shared/logger.js';\n9\t\n10\tconst SENT_AT = '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z';\n11\t\n12\tconst promptBuilder = new PromptBuilder({identity: 'You are a test assistant.'});\n13\t\n14\t// The schema every test below starts from: one required string property.\n15\tconst weatherSchema: Record<string, unknown> = {\n16\t  type: 'object',\n17\t  properties: {location: {type: 'string'}},\n18\t  required: ['location'],\n19\t};\n20\t\n21\tconst registerWeatherTool = (registry: ToolRegistry, schema: Record<string, unknown>, executor: ToolExecutor): void => {\n22\t  registry.register({name: 'get_weather', description: 'weather for a place', inputSchema: schema}, executor);\n23\t};\n24\t\n25\t// Feed the logger's real output path, so a leaked value is caught where an operator would read it.\n26\tconst recordingLogger = (capturedLines: string[]): Logger => {\n27\t  const keep = (category: string, message: string, fields?: Record<string, unknown>): void => {\n28\t    capturedLines.push(JSON.stringify({category, message, fields}));\n29\t  };\n30\t  return {debug: keep, info: keep, warn: keep, error: keep};\n31\t};\n32\t\n33\tdescribe('ToolRegistry input validation', () => {\n34\t  it('rejects a call missing a required property, naming it, without invoking the executor', async () => {\n35\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n36\t    let executorCalls = 0;\n37\t    registerWeatherTool(registry, weatherSchema, async () => {\n38\t      executorCalls += 1;\n39\t      return 'executor ran';\n40\t    });\n41\t\n42\t    const rejection = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n43\t\n44\t    expect({\n45\t      executorCalls,\n46\t      namesRequiredLocation: rejection.result.includes('location') && rejection.result.includes('required'),\n47\t    }).toEqual({executorCalls: 0, namesRequiredLocation: true});\n48\t  });\n49\t\n50\t  it('passes accepted input through to the executor unchanged, exactly once', async () => {\n51\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n52\t    const executorInputs: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n53\t    registerWeatherTool(registry, weatherSchema, async input => {\n54\t      executorInputs.push(input);\n55\t      return 'executor ran';\n56\t    });\n57\t\n58\t    await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Hill End', units: 'metric'});\n59\t\n60\t    expect(executorInputs).toEqual([{location: 'Hill End', units: 'metric'}]);\n61\t  });\n62\t\n63\t  it('rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, never the value', async () => {\n64\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n65\t    let executorCalls = 0;\n66\t    registerWeatherTool(registry, weatherSchema, async () => {\n67\t      executorCalls += 1;\n68\t      return 'executor ran';\n69\t    });\n70\t\n71\t    const rejection = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 4242});\n72\t\n73\t    expect({\n74\t      executorCalls,\n75\t      namesPath: rejection.result.includes('location'),\n76\t      namesExpectedType: rejection.result.includes('string'),\n77\t      carriesValue: rejection.result.includes('4242'),\n78\t    }).toEqual({executorCalls: 0, namesPath: true, namesExpectedType: true, carriesValue: false});\n79\t  });\n80\t\n81\t  it('rejects a value outside an enum without leaking identifying input into result or logs', async () => {\n82\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n83\t    let executorCalls = 0;\n84\t    registerWeatherTool(\n85\t {createChatOrchestrator} from '../chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport {ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\nimport type {ToolExecutor, ToolResponse} from './registry.js';\nimport type {AIProvider, MessageChunk, SendMessageParams} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport {PromptBuilder} from '../../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\nimport {setLogger} from '../../shared/logger.js';\nimport type {Logger} from '../../shared/logger.js';\n\nconst SENT_AT = '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z';\n\nconst promptBuilder = new PromptBuilder({identity: 'You are a test assistant.'});\n\n// The schema every test below starts from: one required string property.\nconst weatherSchema: Record<string, unknown> = {\n  type: 'object',\n  properties: {location: {type: 'string'}},\n  required: ['location'],\n};\n\nconst registerWeatherTool = (registry: ToolRegistry, schema: Record<string, unknown>, executor: ToolExecutor): void => {\n  registry.register({name: 'get_weather', description: 'weather for a place', inputSchema: schema}, executor);\n};\n\n// Feed the logger's real output path, so a leaked value is caught where an operator would read it.\nconst recordingLogger = (capturedLines: string[]): Logger => {\n  const keep = (category: string, message: string, fields?: Record<string, unknown>): void => {\n    capturedLines.push(JSON.stringify({category, message, fields}));\n  };\n  return {debug: keep, info: keep, warn: keep, error: keep};\n};\n\ndescribe('ToolRegistry input validation', () => {\n  it('rejects a call missing a required property, naming it, without invoking the executor', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let executorCalls = 0;\n    registerWeatherTool(registry, weatherSchema, async () => {\n      executorCalls += 1;\n      return 'executor ran';\n    });\n\n    const rejection = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n\n    expect({\n      executorCalls,\n      namesRequiredLocation: rejection.result.includes('location') && rejection.result.includes('required'),\n    }).toEqual({executorCalls: 0, namesRequiredLocation: true});\n  });\n\n  it('passes accepted input through to the executor unchanged, exactly once', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const executorInputs: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n    registerWeatherTool(registry, weatherSchema, async input => {\n      executorInputs.push(input);\n      return 'executor ran';\n    });\n\n    await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Hill End', units: 'metric'});\n\n    expect(executorInputs).toEqual([{location: 'Hill End', units: 'metric'}]);\n  });\n\n  it('rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, never the value', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let executorCalls = 0;\n    registerWeatherTool(registry, weatherSchema, async () => {\n      executorCalls += 1;\n      return 'executor ran';\n    });\n\n    const rejection = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 4242});\n\n    expect({\n      executorCalls,\n      namesPath: rejection.result.includes('location'),\n      namesExpectedType: rejection.result.includes('string'),\n      carriesValue: rejection.result.includes('4242'),\n    }).toEqual({executorCalls: 0, namesPath: true, namesExpectedType: true, carriesValue: false});\n  });\n\n  it('rejects a value outside an enum without leaking identifying input into result or logs', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let executorCalls = 0;\n    registerWeatherTool(\n      registry,\n      {\n        type: 'object',\n        properties: {location: {type: 'string'}, units: {type: 'string', enum: ['celsius', 'fahrenheit']}},\n        required: ['location'],\n      },\n      async () => {\n        executorCalls += 1;\n        return 'executor ran';\n      }\n    );\n\n    const logLines: string[] = [];\n    setLogger(recordingLogger(logLines));\n    let rejection: ToolResponse | undefined;\n    try {\n      rejection = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: '12 Acacia Road', units: 'kelvin'});\n    } finally {\n      setLogger();\n    }\n\n    expect({\n      executorCalls,\n      namesFailedPath: rejection?.result.includes('units') === true,\n      leakedAddress:\n        rejection?.result.includes('12 Acacia Road') === true || logLines.some(line => line.includes('12 Acacia Road')),\n    }).toEqual({executorCalls: 0, namesFailedPath: true, leakedAddress: false});\n  });\n\n  it('refuses an uncompilable schema at registration, naming the tool', () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let thrownMessage: string | undefined;\n    try {\n      registry.register(\n        {\n          name: 'get_weather',\n          description: 'weather for a place',\n          inputSchema: {type: 'object', properties: {location: {type: 'strng'}}},\n        },\n        async () => 'executor ran'\n      );\n    } catch (error) {\n      thrownMessage = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n    }\n\n    expect({threw: thrownMessage !== undefined, namesTool: thrownMessage?.includes('get_weather') === true}).toEqual({\n      threw: true,\n      namesTool: true,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let executorCalls = 0;\n    let currentSchema: Record<string, unknown> = weatherSchema;\n    registry.register(\n      () => ({name: 'get_weather', description: 'weather for a place', inputSchema: currentSchema}),\n      async () => {\n        executorCalls += 1;\n        return 'executor ran';\n      }\n    );\n\n    const first = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Hill End'});\n    const callsAfterFirst = executorCalls;\n    currentSchema = {type: 'object', properties: {year: {type: 'number'}}, required: ['year']};\n    const second = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Hill End'});\n\n    expect({\n      callsAfterFirst,\n      firstResult: first.result,\n      secondRejectedAgainstCurrentSchema: second.result !== 'executor ran' && second.result.includes('year'),\n    }).toEqual({callsAfterFirst: 1, firstResult: 'executor ran', secondRejectedAgainstCurrentSchema: true});\n  });\n\n  it('still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let thrownMessage: string | undefined;\n    try {\n      await registry.execute('no_such_tool', {});\n    } catch (error) {\n      thrownMessage = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n    }\n\n    expect(thrownMessage).toBe('Unknown tool: no_such_tool');\n  });\n\n  it('returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let executorCalls = 0;\n    registerWeatherTool(registry, weatherSchema, async () => {\n      executorCalls += 1;\n      return 'sunny';\n    });\n\n    // One scripted round per sendMessage call; round 1 asks with an empty input, round 2 corrects it.\n    const rounds: MessageChunk[][] = [\n      [\n        {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', name: 'get_weather', input: {}}},\n        {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'},\n      ],\n      [\n        {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-2', name: 'get_weather', input: {location: 'Hill End'}}},\n        {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'},\n      ],\n      [\n        {type: 'text', text: 'the weather in Hill End is sunny'},\n        {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'},\n      ],\n    ];\n    const providerSeen: string[] = [];\n    let round = 0;\n    const provider: AIProvider = {\n      async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n        providerSeen.push(JSON.stringify(params.messages));\n        for (const chunk of rounds[round] ?? []) yield chunk;\n        round += 1;\n      },\n      async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n        return {} as T;\n      },\n    };\n    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(provider, promptBuilder, registry);\n    const session: ChatSession = {\n      sessionId: 's1',\n      userId: 'u1',\n      entries: [{role: 'user', content: 'what is the weather in Hill End', timestamp: SENT_AT}],\n    };\n\n    let text: string | undefined;\n    let failure: unknown;\n    try {\n      text = await orchestrator.processMessage(session);\n    } catch (error) {\n      failure = error;\n    }\n\n    expect({\n      text,\n      threw: failure !== undefined,\n      executorCalls,\n      rejectionReachedProvider: (providerSeen[1] ?? '').includes('required'),\n    }).toEqual({\n      text: 'the weather in Hill End is sunny',\n      threw: false,\n      executorCalls: 1,\n      rejectionReachedProvider: true,\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":235,"startLine":1,"totalLines":235}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_65362cfdfc904620a59e3b40","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_d1a1923bca81411eba5b6ea5","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# HAL Engine tool input validation\n2\t\n3\t| Field  | Value                 |\n4\t| ------ | --------------------- |\n5\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n6\t| Status | In Progress           |\n7\t\n8\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema that is handed verbatim to both providers, and until now nothing checked what the model actually sent: a malformed tool call reached the executor as-is, where each executor improvised its own defence - a cast, a default, a confidently formatted answer for input that was never valid. The registry now validates every call's `input` against the resolved definition's `inputSchema` before the executor runs, so a tool receives only input the schema accepts, and a malformed call comes back to the model as a tool result it can read and correct instead of ending the conversation. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only: it injects no `additionalProperties: false`, coerces no types, applies no defaults, and offers no opt-out.\n9\t\n10\t## Registration\n11\t\n12\t- `register` compiles the resolved definition's `inputSchema` immediately, so a schema no validator accepts throws at registration - at startup, not mid-conversation - and the error names the tool ([validated by: refuses an uncompilable schema at registration, naming the tool](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L114)).\n13\t- A static tool's schema is compiled once at registration and the compiled validator reused across `execute` calls, so ten calls of one tool cost one compilation.\n14\t\n15\t## Validation, before the executor\n16\t\n17\t- `execute` validates the call's `input` against the resolved definition's `inputSchema` before the executor runs: a call the schema rejects returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names what was wrong - a missing required property, for instance - and the executor is never invoked ([validated by: rejects a call missing a required property, naming it, without invoking the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L34)).\n18\t- A field whose value type differs from the declared one is rejected with a message naming the property path and the expected type ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, never the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L63)).\n19\t- An `enum` constraint is enforced like any other: a value outside the declared set is rejected before the executor runs ([validated by: rejects a value outside an enum without leaking identifying input into result or logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L81)).\n20\t- A rejection names failing property paths and the constraint that failed, and carries no property value - not in the returned result, and not in any log line - because tool input is where identifying data enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing ([validated by: rejects a value outside an enum without leaking identifying input into result or logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L81)).\n21\t- Input the schema accepts reaches the executor unchanged, including properties the schema does not declare, exactly once per call: no defaults injected, no types coerced ([validated by: passes accepted input through to the executor unchanged, exactly once](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L50)).\n22\t- An unregistered name still throws `Unknown tool: <name>`, unchanged ([validated by: still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L160)).\n23\t\n24\t## Function-valued sources\n25\t\n26\t- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` is resolved per call, so validation uses the schema the source returns at that call rather than one captured earlier ([validated by: validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L136)).\n27\t\n28\t## The tool loop\n29\t\n30\t- A rejected call is returned to the provider as the tool's result rather than thrown, so `processMessage` resolves and the model can correct the call on a later round within the existing tool-round bound ([validated by: returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L172)).\n31\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"869efe1c-1b30-454f-ace5-d4df21543193","uuid":"9e17bcae-dd2d-4f4f-9da5-6d47dde1adf8","timestamp":"2026-11-03T23:15:26.069Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/5d08507a0ead/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md","content":"# HAL Engine tool input validation\n\n| Field  | Value                 |\n| ------ | --------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n| Status | In Progress           |\n\nEvery tool declares a JSON Schema that is handed verbatim to both providers, and until now nothing checked what the model actually sent: a malformed tool call reached the executor as-is, where each executor improvised its own defence - a cast, a default, a confidently formatted answer for input that was never valid. The registry now validates every call's `input` against the resolved definition's `inputSchema` before the executor runs, so a tool receives only input the schema accepts, and a malformed call comes back to the model as a tool result it can read and correct instead of ending the conversation. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only: it injects no `additionalProperties: false`, coerces no types, applies no defaults, and offers no opt-out.\n\n## Registration\n\n- `register` compiles the resolved definition's `inputSchema` immediately, so a schema no validator accepts throws at registration - at startup, not mid-conversation - and the error names the tool ([validated by: refuses an uncompilable schema at registration, naming the tool](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L114)).\n- A static tool's schema is compiled once at registration and the compiled validator reused across `execute` calls, so ten calls of one tool cost one compilation.\n\n## Validation, before the executor\n\n- `execute` validates the call's `input` against the resolved definition's `inputSchema` before the executor runs: a call the schema rejects returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names what was wrong - a missing required property, for instance - and the executor is never invoked ([validated by: rejects a call missing a required property, naming it, without invoking the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L34)).\n- A field whose value type differs from the declared one is rejected with a message naming the property path and the expected type ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, never the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L63)).\n- An `enum` constraint is enforced like any other: a value outside the declared set is rejected before the executor runs ([validated by: rejects a value outside an enum without leaking identifying input into result or logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L81)).\n- A rejection names failing property paths and the constraint that failed, and carries no property value - not in the returned result, and not in any log line - because tool input is where identifying data enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing ([validated by: rejects a value outside an enum without leaking identifying input into result or logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L81)).\n- Input the schema accepts reaches the executor unchanged, including properties the schema does not declare, exactly once per call: no defaults injected, no types coerced ([validated by: passes accepted input through to the executor unchanged, exactly once](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L50)).\n- An unregistered name still throws `Unknown tool: <name>`, unchanged ([validated by: still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L160)).\n\n## Function-valued sources\n\n- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` is resolved per call, so validation uses the schema the source returns at that call rather than one captured earlier ([validated by: validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L136)).\n\n## The tool loop\n\n- A rejected call is returned to the provider as the tool's result rather than thrown, so `processMessage` resolves and the model can correct the call on a later round within the existing tool-round bound ([validated by: returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L172)).\n","numLines":31,"startLine":1,"totalLines":31}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_d1a1923bca81411eba5b6ea5","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
